    20→
    21→/// Direction(s) indicated by `..` notation.
    22→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    23→pub enum DotDirection {
    24→    /// No dots: just the revision itself.
    25→    None,
    26→    /// `x..` — ancestors (x at top, older below).
    27→    Ancestors,
    28→    /// `..x` — descendants (newer above, x at bottom).
    29→    Descendants,
    30→    /// `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle.
    31→    Both,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
    35→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    36→pub struct DotSpec {
    37→    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
    38→    pub rev: String,
    39→    /// Which direction(s) the dots indicate.
    40→    pub direction: DotDirection,
    41→    /// Count per dotted side (x is not counted).
    42→    pub count: usize,
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
    46→///
    47→/// Returns the bare revision, direction, and default count (0).
    48→/// Count is set separately from the second positional arg.
    49→pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
    50→    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
    51→        DotSpec {
    52→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    53→            direction: DotDirection::Both,
    54→            count: 0,
    55→        }
    56→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
    57→        DotSpec {
    58→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    59→            direction: DotDirection::Descendants,
    60→            count: 0,
    61→        }
    62→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
    63→        DotSpec {
    64→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    65→            direction: DotDirection::Ancestors,
    66→            count: 0,
    67→        }
    68→    } else {
    69→        DotSpec {
    70→            rev: rev.to_string(),
    71→            direction: DotDirection::None,
    72→            count: 0,
    73→        }
    74→    }
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Resolve effective revision, limit, and whether results need reversing
    78→/// from positional args combined with named flags.
    79→///
    80→/// Named flags (`-r`, `-l`) take precedence over positional args.
    81→pub struct ResolvedArgs {
    82→    /// The jj revset string to evaluate.
    83→    pub revset: String,
    84→    /// Maximum number of commits to return (None = unlimited).
    85→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    86→    /// For `..x..`, the count per side.
    87→    pub both_count: Option<usize>,
    88→    /// The bare primary revision (for highlighting).
    89→    pub primary_rev: String,
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Resolve positional `..` args with named flag overrides.
    93→///
    94→/// - `pos_rev`: first positional arg (may contain `..`)
    95→/// - `pos_count`: second positional arg
    96→/// - `flag_rev`: `-r` flag value (if different from default)
    97→/// - `flag_limit`: `-l` flag value
    98→/// - `default_rev`: the default revision (e.g. "@")
    99→pub fn resolve_dot_args(
   100→    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
   101→    pos_count: Option<usize>,
   102→    flag_rev: &str,
   103→    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
   104→    default_rev: &str,
   105→) -> ResolvedArgs {
   106→    // If named flags were explicitly set, they take full precedence
   107→    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
   108→    let flag_limit_set = flag_limit.is_some();
   109→
   110→    if flag_rev_set || (flag_limit_set && pos_rev.is_none()) {
   111→        return ResolvedArgs {
   112→            revset: flag_rev.to_string(),
   113→            limit: flag_limit,
   114→            both_count: None,
   115→            primary_rev: flag_rev.to_string(),
   116→        };
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    // Use positional args
   120→    let rev_str = pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev);
   121→    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
   122→
   123→    // If there's a positional count, set it
   124→    if let Some(c) = pos_count {
   125→        spec.count = c;
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    // Named limit overrides positional count
   129→    if flag_limit_set {
   130→        spec.count = flag_limit.unwrap();
   131→    }
   132→
   133→    let primary = spec.rev.clone();
   134→
   135→    match spec.direction {
   136→        DotDirection::None => {
   137→            if spec.count > 0 {
   138→                // bare `x 5` → `x.. 5` (ancestors)
   139→                ResolvedArgs {
   140→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   141→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1), // +1 for x itself
   142→                    both_count: None,
   143→                    primary_rev: primary,
   144→                }
   145→            } else {
   146→                ResolvedArgs {
   147→                    revset: spec.rev,
   148→                    limit: Some(1),
   149→                    both_count: None,
   150→                    primary_rev: primary,
   151→                }
   152→            }
   153→        }
   154→        DotDirection::Ancestors => {
   155→            if spec.count > 0 {
   156→                ResolvedArgs {
   157→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   158→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
   159→                    both_count: None,
   160→                    primary_rev: primary,
   161→                }
   162→            } else {
   163→                ResolvedArgs {
   164→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   165→                    limit: None,
   166→                    both_count: None,
   167→                    primary_rev: primary,
   168→                }
   169→            }
   170→        }
   171→        DotDirection::Descendants => {
   172→            if spec.count > 0 {
   173→                ResolvedArgs {
   174→                    revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
   175→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
   176→                    both_count: None,
   177→                    primary_rev: primary,
   178→                }
   179→            } else {
   180→                ResolvedArgs {
   181→                    revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
   182→                    limit: None,
   183→                    both_count: None,
   184→                    primary_rev: primary,
   185→                }
   186→            }
   187→        }
   188→        DotDirection::Both => ResolvedArgs {
   189→            revset: format!("::{}|{}::", spec.rev, spec.rev),
   190→            limit: None,
   191→            both_count: Some(spec.count),
   192→            primary_rev: primary,
   193→        },
   194→    }
   195→}
   196→
   197→/// Wrap text in ANSI bold escape codes.
   198→pub fn bold(s: &str) -> String {
   199→    format!("\x1b[1m{s}\x1b[0m")
   200→}
   201→
   202→/// Bold only the first line of a multi-line string.
   203→pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
   204→    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
   205→        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
   206→    } else {
   207→        bold(s)
   208→    }
   209→}
   210→
   211→/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
   212→///
   213→/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
   214→pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
   215→    if n == 0 {
   216→        return s.to_string();
   217→    }
   218→    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
   219→    let mut lines = s.lines();
   220→    let mut result = match lines.next() {
   221→        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
   222→        None => return String::new(),
   223→    };
   224→    for line in lines {
   225→        result.push('\n');
   226→        if !line.is_empty() {
   227→            result.push_str(&pad);
   228→        }
   229→        result.push_str(line);
   230→    }
   231→    result
   232→}
   233→
   234→/// Format: just the short changeID.
   235→pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   236→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   237→    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
   238→}
   239→
   240→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
   241→pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   242→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   243→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   244→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   245→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   246→    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   247→    if first_line.is_empty() {
   248→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   249→    } else {
   250→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
   251→    }
   252→}
   253→
   254→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
   255→pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   256→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   257→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   258→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   259→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   260→    let desc = commit.description();
   261→    if desc.is_empty() {
   262→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   263→    } else {
   264→        let mut lines = desc.lines();
   265→        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
   266→        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
   267→        for line in lines {
   268→            result.push('\n');
   269→            result.push_str(line);
   270→        }
   271→        result
   272→    }
   273→}
   274→
   275→/// Collect formatted lines for `..x..` (both directions).
   276→///
   277→/// `formatter` converts a `Commit` into a display string.
   278→/// Returns `(lines, anchor_index)` — the lines in display order
   279→/// (descendants newest first, anchor, ancestors newest first)
   280→/// and the index of the anchor commit within the lines.
   281→pub fn collect_both<F>(
   282→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   283→    repo_path: &Path,
   284→    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
   285→    both_count: usize,
   286→    formatter: F,
   287→) -> Result<(Vec<String>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
   288→where
   289→    F: Fn(&Commit) -> String,
   290→{
   291→    let bare_rev = pos_rev.unwrap_or("@");
   292→    let spec = parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);
   293→    let (workspace, _) = load_repo(repo_path)?;
   294→    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &spec.rev)?;
   295→    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
   296→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", spec.rev).into());
   297→    }
   298→    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
   299→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   300→
   301→    let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("::{}", spec.rev))?;
   302→    let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("{}::", spec.rev))?;
   303→
   304→    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   305→    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   306→    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {
   307→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   308→            continue;
   309→        }
   310→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   311→        desc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));
   312→    }
   313→    if both_count > 0 {
   314→        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
   315→        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();
   316→    } else {
   317→        desc_lines.clear();
   318→    }
   319→
   320→    // Anchor
   321→    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;
   322→
   323→    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   324→    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   325→    if both_count > 0 {
   326→        let mut count = 0;
   327→        for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {
   328→            if root_commit_id == *commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   329→                continue;
   330→            }
   331→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   332→            anc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));
   333→            count += 1;
   334→            if count >= both_count {
   335→                break;
   336→            }
   337→        }
   338→    }
   339→
   340→    let anchor_index = desc_lines.len();
   341→    let mut result = desc_lines;
   342→    result.push(formatter(&anchor_commit));
   343→    result.extend(anc_lines);
   344→    Ok((result, anchor_index))
   345→}
   346→
   347→pub fn load_repo(
   348→    path: &Path,
   349→) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   350→    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
   351→    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
   352→    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
   353→    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
   354→
   355→    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
   356→    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
   357→
   358→    Ok((workspace, repo))
   359→}
   360→
   361→/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
   362→pub fn resolve_revset(
   363→    workspace: &Workspace,
   364→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   365→    revset_str: &str,
   366→) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   367→    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
   368→    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
   369→    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
   370→    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
   371→    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
   372→        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
   373→        base: workspace_root,
   374→    };
   375→    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
   376→    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
   377→        path_converter: &path_converter,
   378→        workspace_name,
   379→    };
   380→    let context = RevsetParseContext {
   381→        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
   382→        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
   383→        user_email: "",
   384→        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
   385→        default_ignored_remote: None,
   386→        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
   387→        use_glob_by_default: false,
   388→        extensions: &extensions,
   389→        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
   390→    };
   391→
   392→    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
   393→    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
   394→
   395→    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
   396→    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
   397→    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
   398→    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
   399→
   400→    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
   401→    for result in revset.iter() {
   402→        commit_ids.push(result?);
   403→    }
   404→    Ok(commit_ids)
   405→}

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
     5→
     6→use crate::common;
     7→
     8→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     9→pub struct DescArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg()]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Count (items per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg()]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to describe (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    24→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    25→
    26→    /// Maximum number of commits to describe
    27→    #[arg(short, long)]
    28→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    29→
    30→    /// Number of spaces to indent description body lines (default: 3)
    31→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "3")]
    32→    pub indent: usize,
    33→}
    34→
    35→pub fn desc(args: &DescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    36→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    37→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    38→        args.pos_count,
    39→        &args.revision,
    40→        args.limit,
    41→        "@",
    42→    );
    43→
    44→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    45→
    46→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    47→
    48→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    49→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    50→    }
    51→
    52→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    53→
    54→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    55→        let (lines, anchor) = common::collect_both(
    56→            &repo,
    57→            &args.repo,
    58→            args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    59→            both_count,
    60→            common::format_commit_full,
    61→        )?;
    62→        for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
    63→            let indented = common::indent_body(line, args.indent);
    64→            if i == anchor {
    65→                println!("{}", common::bold_first_line(&indented));
    66→            } else {
    67→                println!("{indented}");
    68→            }
    69→        }
    70→        return Ok(());
    71→    }
    72→
    73→    // Resolve the primary commit ID for highlighting
    74→    let primary_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.primary_rev)?;
    75→    let primary_id = primary_ids.first();
    76→
    77→    let mut results: Vec<(String, bool)> = Vec::new();
    78→    let mut count = 0;
    79→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
    80→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    81→            continue;
    82→        }
    83→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    84→        let is_primary = primary_id == Some(commit_id);
    85→        results.push((common::format_commit_full(&commit), is_primary));
    86→
    87→        count += 1;
    88→        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit
    89→            && count >= limit
    90→        {
    91→            break;
    92→        }
    93→    }
    94→
    95→    for (line, is_primary) in &results {
    96→        let indented = common::indent_body(line, args.indent);
    97→        if *is_primary {
    98→            println!("{}", common::bold_first_line(&indented));
    99→        } else {
   100→            println!("{indented}");
   101→        }
   102→    }
   103→
   104→    Ok(())
   105→}
   106→
```

   108→    pub fn remove_wc_commit(&mut self, name: &WorkspaceName) {

   110→    }
   111→
   112→    pub fn rename_workspace(
   113→        &mut self,
   114→        old_name: &WorkspaceName,
   115→        new_name: WorkspaceNameBuf,
   116→    ) -> Result<(), RenameWorkspaceError> {
   117→        if self.data.wc_commit_ids.contains_key(&new_name) {
   118→            return Err(RenameWorkspaceError::WorkspaceAlreadyExists {
   119→                name: new_name.clone(),
   120→            });
   121→        }
   122→        let wc_commit_id = self.data.wc_commit_ids.remove(old_name).ok_or_else(|| {
   123→            RenameWorkspaceError::WorkspaceDoesNotExist {
   124→                name: old_name.to_owned(),
   125→            }
   126→        })?;
   127→        self.data.wc_commit_ids.insert(new_name, wc_commit_id);
   128→        Ok(())
   129→    }
   130→
   131→    pub fn add_head(&mut self, head_id: &CommitId) {

   133→    }

   139→    /// Iterates local bookmark `(name, target)`s in lexicographical order.
   140→    pub fn local_bookmarks(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&RefName, &RefTarget)> {
   141→        self.data
   142→            .local_bookmarks
   143→            .iter()
   144→            .map(|(name, target)| (name.as_ref(), target))
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    /// Iterates local bookmarks `(name, target)` in lexicographical order where
   148→    /// the target adds `commit_id`.
   149→    pub fn local_bookmarks_for_commit(
   150→        &self,
   151→        commit_id: &CommitId,
   152→    ) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&RefName, &RefTarget)> {
   153→        self.local_bookmarks()
   154→            .filter(|(_, target)| target.added_ids().contains(commit_id))
   155→    }
   156→
   157→    /// Iterates local bookmark `(name, target)`s matching the given pattern.
   158→    /// Entries are sorted by `name`.
   159→    pub fn local_bookmarks_matching(
   160→        &self,
   161→        matcher: &StringMatcher,
   162→    ) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&RefName, &RefTarget)> {
   163→        matcher
   164→            .filter_btree_map_as_deref(&self.data.local_bookmarks)
   165→            .map(|(name, target)| (name.as_ref(), target))
   166→    }

OK, I have everything I need. Here's my plan:

## `show` subcommand plan

**Version bump**: 0.17.0 → 0.18.0

**Scope**: Reproduce `jj show` output using jj-lib directly:
- Commit ID (full hex)
- Change ID (full reverse hex)
- Bookmarks (local + remote, via `view.bookmarks()` matching commit ID)
- Author / Committer (name, email, timestamp formatted with tz offset)
- Description (indented 4 spaces, matching jj's style)
- Diff summary: "Added/Modified/Removed regular file <path>"

The diff **summary** (file list with status) is feasible without new deps — we just compare `tree_ids` at each directory entry. The actual **diff content** (line-by-line unified diff of file contents) would be a follow-up.

**Approach**: I'd recommend **single-step** since this follows the established pattern (desc.rs is a close template), the jj-lib APIs are straightforward, and no new crate deps are needed. The `Timestamp` is `millis_since_epoch` + `tz_offset` in minutes — we already have `chrono` for formatting.

Does single-step sound right, or would you prefer multi-step (e.g., dev0 for header, dev1 for diff summary)?

Good — `Diff<MergedTreeValue>` has `.before` and `.after`, each is `Merge<Option<TreeValue>>`. I can check `is_absent()` / `as_resolved()` to determine Added/Modified/Removed, and match on `TreeValue` variants for the file type string.

Now I need to figure out the async story. `diff_stream` returns a `BoxStream` which needs `futures::StreamExt`. Let me check if `TreeDiffIterator::new` is usable directly as a sync path.

```
   260→    fn diff_stream_internal<'matcher>(
   261→        &self,
   262→        other: &Self,
   263→        matcher: &'matcher dyn Matcher,
   264→    ) -> TreeDiffStream<'matcher> {
   265→        let concurrency = self.store().concurrency();
   266→        if concurrency <= 1 {
   267→            Box::pin(futures::stream::iter(TreeDiffIterator::new(
   268→                self, other, matcher,
   269→            )))
   270→        } else {
   271→            Box::pin(TreeDiffStreamImpl::new(self, other, matcher, concurrency))
   272→        }
   273→    }
   274→
```

> AGENT

`TreeDiffIterator::new` is public and implements `Iterator` — perfect, I can use it directly without async/futures. Since the local git backend has concurrency=1, this is exactly what jj itself would do.

     9→pub struct ChidArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg()]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Count (items per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg()]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to look up (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    24→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    25→
    26→    /// Maximum number of changeIDs to show
    27→    #[arg(short, long)]
    28→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    29→}
    30→
    31→pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    32→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    33→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    34→        args.pos_count,
    35→        &args.revision,
    36→        args.limit,
    37→        "@",
    38→    );
    39→
    40→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    41→
    42→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    43→
    44→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    45→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    49→
    50→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    51→        let (lines, anchor) = common::collect_both(
    52→            &repo,
    53→            &args.repo,
    54→            args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    55→            both_count,
    56→            common::format_chid,
    57→        )?;
    58→        for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
    59→            if i == anchor {
    60→                println!("{}", common::bold(line));
    61→            } else {
    62→                println!("{line}");
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        return Ok(());
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // Resolve the primary commit ID for highlighting
    69→    let primary_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.primary_rev)?;
    70→    let primary_id = primary_ids.first();
    71→
    72→    let mut results: Vec<(String, bool)> = Vec::new();
    73→    let mut count = 0;
    74→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
    75→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    76→            continue;
    77→        }
    78→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    79→        let is_primary = primary_id == Some(commit_id);
    80→        results.push((common::format_chid(&commit), is_primary));
    81→
    82→        count += 1;
    83→        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit
    84→            && count >= limit
    85→        {
    86→            break;
    87→        }
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    for (line, is_primary) in &results {
    91→        if *is_primary {
    92→            println!("{}", common::bold(line));
    93→        } else {
    94→            println!("{line}");
    95→        }
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    Ok(())
    99→}
   100→

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "content": "use std::path::PathBuf;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;\nuse jj_lib::commit::Commit;\nuse jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex;\nuse jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;\nuse jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;\nuse jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;\nuse jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};\nuse pollster::FutureExt;\n\nuse crate::common;\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct ShowArgs {\n    /// Revision to show (default: @)\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n}\n\npub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", args.revision).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let commit_id = &commit_ids[0];\n    if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n        return Err(\"cannot show root commit\".into());\n    }\n\n    let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n\n    print_header(&commit, &repo);\n    print_description(&commit);\n    print_diff(&commit, &repo)?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn print_header(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) {\n    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n\n    println!(\"Commit ID: {commit_hex}\");\n    println!(\"Change ID: {change_hex}\");\n\n    // Bookmarks\n    let bookmark_str = format_bookmarks(commit, repo);\n    if !bookmark_str.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"Bookmarks: {bookmark_str}\");\n    }\n\n    // Author\n    let author = commit.author();\n    println!(\n        \"Author   : {} <{}> ({})\",\n        author.name,\n        author.email,\n        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)\n    );\n\n    // Committer\n    let committer = commit.committer();\n    println!(\n        \"Committer: {} <{}> ({})\",\n        committer.name,\n        committer.email,\n        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)\n    );\n}\n\nfn format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) -> String {\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {\n        // Local bookmark\n        if local_remote.local_target.added_ids().contains(commit.id()) {\n            names.push(name.to_string());\n        }\n        // Remote bookmarks\n        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {\n            if remote_ref.target.added_ids().contains(commit.id()) {\n                names.push(format!(\"{name}@{remote_name}\"));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    names.join(\" \")\n}\n\nfn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {\n    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;\n    let secs = millis / 1000;\n    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;\n    let tz_hours = tz_minutes / 60;\n    let tz_mins = (tz_minutes % 60).abs();\n\n    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)\n        .unwrap_or_default()\n        .with_timezone(&chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()));\n\n    dt.format(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S\").to_string()\n}\n\nfn print_description(commit: &Commit) {\n    let desc = commit.description();\n    println!();\n    if desc.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"    (no description set)\");\n    } else {\n        for line in desc.lines() {\n            println!(\"    {line}\");\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}\n\nfn print_diff(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo).block_on()?;\n    let commit_tree = commit.tree();\n\n    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);\n\n    for entry in diff_iter {\n        let path = entry.path;\n        let diff = entry.values?;\n        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n\n        match (before, after) {\n            (None, Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"Added {} {}:\", file_type_str(&value), path);\n            }\n            (Some(_), None) => {\n                println!(\"Removed {} {}:\", file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before), path);\n            }\n            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"Modified {} {}:\", file_type_str(&value), path);\n            }\n            (None, None) => {\n                // Conflicted or both absent — skip\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {\n    match value {\n        TreeValue::File { executable: false, .. } => \"regular file\",\n        TreeValue::File { executable: true, .. } => \"executable file\",\n        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => \"symlink\",\n        TreeValue::Tree(_) => \"tree\",\n        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => \"git submodule\",\n    }\n}\n\nfn file_type_str_from_merged(\n    merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>,\n) -> &'static str {\n    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {\n        file_type_str(value)\n    } else {\n        \"file\"\n    }\n}\n"
}

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;
     5→use jj_lib::commit::Commit;
     6→use jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex;
     7→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
     8→use jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;
     9→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
    10→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
    11→use pollster::FutureExt;
    12→
    13→use crate::common;
    14→
    15→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    16→pub struct ShowArgs {
    17→    /// Revision to show (default: @)
    18→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    19→    pub revision: String,
    20→
    21→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    22→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    23→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    24→}
    25→
    26→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    27→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    28→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;
    29→
    30→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    31→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", args.revision).into());
    32→    }
    33→
    34→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    35→    let commit_id = &commit_ids[0];
    36→    if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    37→        return Err("cannot show root commit".into());
    38→    }
    39→
    40→    let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    41→
    42→    print_header(&commit, &repo);
    43→    print_description(&commit);
    44→    print_diff(&commit, &repo)?;
    45→
    46→    Ok(())
    47→}
    48→
    49→fn print_header(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) {
    50→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
    51→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
    52→
    53→    println!("Commit ID: {commit_hex}");
    54→    println!("Change ID: {change_hex}");
    55→
    56→    // Bookmarks
    57→    let bookmark_str = format_bookmarks(commit, repo);
    58→    if !bookmark_str.is_empty() {
    59→        println!("Bookmarks: {bookmark_str}");
    60→    }
    61→
    62→    // Author
    63→    let author = commit.author();
    64→    println!(
    65→        "Author   : {} <{}> ({})",
    66→        author.name,
    67→        author.email,
    68→        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)
    69→    );
    70→
    71→    // Committer
    72→    let committer = commit.committer();
    73→    println!(
    74→        "Committer: {} <{}> ({})",
    75→        committer.name,
    76→        committer.email,
    77→        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)
    78→    );
    79→}
    80→
    81→fn format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) -> String {
    82→    let view = repo.view();
    83→    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    84→
    85→    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {
    86→        // Local bookmark
    87→        if local_remote.local_target.added_ids().contains(commit.id()) {
    88→            names.push(name.to_string());
    89→        }
    90→        // Remote bookmarks
    91→        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {
    92→            if remote_ref.target.added_ids().contains(commit.id()) {
    93→                names.push(format!("{name}@{remote_name}"));
    94→            }
    95→        }
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    names.join(" ")
    99→}
   100→
   101→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   102→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   103→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   104→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   105→    let tz_hours = tz_minutes / 60;
   106→    let tz_mins = (tz_minutes % 60).abs();
   107→
   108→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   109→        .unwrap_or_default()
   110→        .with_timezone(
   111→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   112→        );
   113→
   114→    dt.format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").to_string()
   115→}
   116→
   117→fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {
   118→    let desc = commit.description();
   119→    println!();
   120→    if desc.is_empty() {
   121→        println!("    (no description set)");
   122→    } else {
   123→        for line in desc.lines() {
   124→            println!("    {line}");
   125→        }
   126→    }
   127→    println!();
   128→}
   129→
   130→fn print_diff(commit: &Commit, repo: &impl Repo) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   131→    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo).block_on()?;
   132→    let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   133→
   134→    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   135→
   136→    for entry in diff_iter {
   137→        let path = entry.path;
   138→        let diff = entry.values?;
   139→        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   140→        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   141→
   142→        match (before, after) {
   143→            (None, Some(value)) => {
   144→                println!("Added {} {}:", file_type_str(&value), path);
   145→            }
   146→            (Some(_), None) => {
   147→                println!(
   148→                    "Removed {} {}:",
   149→                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),
   150→                    path
   151→                );
   152→            }
   153→            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {
   154→                println!("Modified {} {}:", file_type_str(&value), path);
   155→            }
   156→            (None, None) => {
   157→                // Conflicted or both absent — skip
   158→            }
   159→        }
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    Ok(())
   163→}
   164→
   165→fn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {
   166→    match value {
   167→        TreeValue::File {
   168→            executable: false, ..
   169→        } => "regular file",
   170→        TreeValue::File {
   171→            executable: true, ..
   172→        } => "executable file",
   173→        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => "symlink",
   174→        TreeValue::Tree(_) => "tree",
   175→        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => "git submodule",
   176→    }
   177→}
   178→
   179→fn file_type_str_from_merged(merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>) -> &'static str {
   180→    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {
   181→        file_type_str(value)
   182→    } else {
   183→        "file"
   184→    }
   185→}
   186→

format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> String {\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() > DEVELOPER

Let's flesh out the "header" information "from Commit ID .. Commiter" to match the format from gitk instead of `jj show` that is:
Author: ...
Committer: ...
Parent: <changeid> <commitid> <title> # there maybe multiple "Parent: ..."
Child: <changeid> <commitid> <title> # there maybe multiple "Child: ..."
Bookmarks: <bm1>, <bm2> ...
Follows: <?> # I assume <changeid> <commitid> but I'm not sure what this is so far it's empty
Precedes: <?> # same as Follows?

> AGENT

In gitk, "Follows" and "Precedes" refer to **git tags** — the nearest ancestor tag and nearest descendant tag respectively (like `git describe`). They're empty when there are no tags, which is likely your case.

For the implementation — this would be version 0.19.0. I'd recommend **single-step** since it's just reworking the header format in `show.rs`:

- Parent/Child lines: parents from `commit.parent_ids()`, children via `children()` revset
- Author/Committer: keep current format
- Bookmarks: comma-separated
- Follows/Precedes: from git tags via `view.tags()` and ancestry checks

Single-step good?

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use std::sync::Arc;
     3→
     4→use chrono::Offset;
     5→use clap::Args;
     6→use jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;
     7→use jj_lib::commit::Commit;
     8→use jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex;
     9→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
    10→use jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;
    11→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
    12→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
    13→use pollster::FutureExt;
    14→
    15→use crate::common;
    16→
    17→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    18→pub struct ShowArgs {
    19→    /// Revision to show (default: @)
    20→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    21→    pub revision: String,
    22→
    23→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    24→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    25→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    26→}
    27→
    28→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    29→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    30→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;
    31→
    32→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    33→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", args.revision).into());
    34→    }
    35→
    36→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    37→    let commit_id = &commit_ids[0];
    38→    if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    39→        return Err("cannot show root commit".into());
    40→    }
    41→
    42→    let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    43→
    44→    print_header(&commit, &repo);
    45→    print_description(&commit);
    46→    print_diff(&commit, &repo)?;
    47→
    48→    Ok(())
    49→}
    50→
    51→fn print_header(commit: &Commit, repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) {
    52→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
    53→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
    54→
    55→    println!("Commit ID: {commit_hex}");
    56→    println!("Change ID: {change_hex}");
    57→
    58→    // Bookmarks
    59→    let bookmark_str = format_bookmarks(commit, repo);
    60→    if !bookmark_str.is_empty() {
    61→        println!("Bookmarks: {bookmark_str}");
    62→    }
    63→
    64→    // Author
    65→    let author = commit.author();
    66→    println!(
    67→        "Author   : {} <{}> ({})",
    68→        author.name,
    69→        author.email,
    70→        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)
    71→    );
    72→
    73→    // Committer
    74→    let committer = commit.committer();
    75→    println!(
    76→        "Committer: {} <{}> ({})",
    77→        committer.name,
    78→        committer.email,
    79→        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)
    80→    );
    81→}
    82→
    83→fn format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> String {
    84→    let view = repo.view();
    85→    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    86→
    87→    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {
    88→        // Local bookmark
    89→        if local_remote
    90→            .local_target
    91→            .added_ids()
    92→            .any(|id| id == commit.id())
    93→        {
    94→            names.push(name.as_str().to_string());
    95→        }
    96→        // Remote bookmarks
    97→        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {
    98→            if remote_ref.target.added_ids().any(|id| id == commit.id()) {
    99→                names.push(format!("{}@{}", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));
   100→            }
   101→        }
   102→    }
   103→
   104→    names.join(" ")
   105→}
   106→
   107→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   108→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   109→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   110→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   111→
   112→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   113→        .unwrap_or_default()
   114→        .with_timezone(
   115→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   116→        );
   117→
   118→    dt.format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").to_string()
   119→}
   120→
   121→fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {
   122→    let desc = commit.description();
   123→    println!();
   124→    if desc.is_empty() {
   125→        println!("    (no description set)");
   126→    } else {
   127→        for line in desc.lines() {
   128→            println!("    {line}");
   129→        }
   130→    }
   131→    println!();
   132→}
   133→
   134→fn print_diff(
   135→    commit: &Commit,
   136→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   137→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   138→    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo.as_ref()).block_on()?;
   139→    let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   140→
   141→    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   142→
   143→    for entry in diff_iter {
   144→        let diff = entry.values?;
   145→        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   146→        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   147→
   148→        // Skip tree (directory) entries — only show files
   149→        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))
   150→            || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))
   151→        {
   152→            continue;
   153→        }
   154→
   155→        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string();
   156→
   157→        match (before, after) {
   158→            (None, Some(value)) => {
   159→                println!("Added {} {path_str}:", file_type_str(&value));
   160→            }
   161→            (Some(_), None) => {
   162→                println!(
   163→                    "Removed {} {path_str}:",
   164→                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),
   165→                );
   166→            }
   167→            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {
   168→                println!("Modified {} {path_str}:", file_type_str(&value));
   169→            }
   170→            (None, None) => {
   171→                // Conflicted or both absent — skip
   172→            }
   173→        }
   174→    }
   175→
   176→    Ok(())
   177→}
   178→
   179→fn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {
   180→    match value {
   181→        TreeValue::File {
   182→            executable: false, ..
   183→        } => "regular file",
   184→        TreeValue::File {
   185→            executable: true, ..
   186→        } => "executable file",
   187→        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => "symlink",
   188→        TreeValue::Tree(_) => "tree",
   189→        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => "git submodule",
   190→    }
   191→}
   192→
   193→fn file_type_str_from_merged(merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>) -> &'static str {
   194→    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {
   195→        file_type_str(value)
   196→    } else {
   197→        "file"
   198→    }
   199→}
   200→

print_header(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    workspace: &Workspace,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    // Author\n    let author = commit.author();\n repo, false)?;\n    println!(\"Precedes:  {}\", precedes.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"\"));\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn find_nearest_tag(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    ancestor: is ancestor of commit (tag comes before)\n                index.is_ancestor(tag_commit_id, commit_id)?\n            } else {\n                // Tag is descendant of commit (tag comes after)\n generation number as distance proxy\n            let tag_entry = index.entry_by_id(tag_commit_id);\n            let commit_entry = index.entry_by_id(commit_id);\n            let distance = if let (Ok(te), Ok(ce)) = (tag_entry, commit_entry) {\n                let tg = te.generation_number();\n                let cg = ce.generation_number();\n                if tg > cg { tg - cg } else { cg - tg }\n            } else {\n                continue;\n            };\n\n            if best.is_none() || distance < best.as_ref().unwrap().1 {\n                best = Some((name.as_str().to_string(), distance));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(best.map(|(name, _)| name))\n}\n\nfn format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> String {\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {\n        // Local bookmark\n        if local_remote\n            .local_target\n            .added_ids()\n            .any(|id| id == commit.id())\n        {\n            names.push(name.as_str().to_string());\n        }\n        // Remote bookmarks\n        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {\n            if remote_ref.target.added_ids().any(|id| id == commit.id()) {\n                names.push(format!(\"{}@{}\", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    names.join(\", \")\n}\n\nfn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {\n    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;\n    let secs = millis / 1000;\n    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;\n\n    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)\n        .unwrap_or_default()\n        .with_timezone(\n            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),\n        );\n\n    dt.format(\"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S\").to_string()\n}\n\nfn print_description(commit: &Commit) {\n    let desc = commit.description();\n    println!();\n    if desc.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"    (no description set)\");\n    } else {\n        for line in desc.lines() {\n            println!(\"    {line}\");\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}\n\nfn print_diff(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo.as_ref()).block_on()?;\n    let commit_tree = commit.tree();\n\n    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);\n\n    for entry in diff_iter {\n        let diff = entry.values?;\n        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n\n        // Skip tree (directory) entries — only show files\n        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n            || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n        {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string();\n\n        match (before, after) {\n            (None, Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"Added {} {path_str}:\", file_type_str(&value));\n            }\n            (Some(_), None) => {\n                println!(\n                    \"Removed {} {path_str}:\",\n                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),\n                );\n            }\n            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"Modified {} {path_str}:\", file_type_str(&value));\n            }\n            (None, None) => {\n                // Conflicted or both absent — skip\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {\n    match value {\n        TreeValue::File {\n            executable: false, ..\n        } => \"regular file\",\n        TreeValue::File {\n            executable: true, ..\n        } => \"executable file\",\n        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => \"symlink\",\n        TreeValue::Tree(_) => \"tree\",\n        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => \"git submodule\",\n    }\n}\n\nfn file_type_str_from_merged(merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>) -> &'static str {\n    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {\n        file_type_str(value)\n    } else {\n        \"file\"\n    }\n}\n"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "old_string": "fn find_nearest_tag(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    ancestor: bool,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let index = repo.index();\n    let commit_id = commit.id();\n\n    let mut best: Option<(String, u64)> = None;\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.tags() {\n        for tag_commit_id in local_remote.local_target.added_ids() {\n            let is_related = if ancestor {\n                // Tag is ancestor of commit (tag comes before)\n                index.is_ancestor(tag_commit_id, commit_id)?\n            } else {\n                // Tag is descendant of commit (tag comes after)\n                index.is_ancestor(commit_id, tag_commit_id)?\n            };\n\n            if !is_related || tag_commit_id == commit_id {\n                continue;\n            }\n\n            // Use generation number as distance proxy\n            let tag_entry = index.entry_by_id(tag_commit_id);\n            let commit_entry = index.entry_by_id(commit_id);\n            let distance = if let (Ok(te), Ok(ce)) = (tag_entry, commit_entry) {\n                let tg = te.generation_number();\n                let cg = ce.generation_number();\n                if tg > cg { tg - cg } else { cg - tg }\n            } else {\n                continue;\n            };\n\n            if best.is_none() || distance < best.as_ref().unwrap().1 {\n                best = Some((name.as_str().to_string(), distance));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(best.map(|(name, _)| name))\n}",
  "new_string": "fn find_nearest_tag(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    workspace: &Workspace,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    ancestor: bool,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n\n    // \"follows\": nearest ancestor tag = heads(tags() & ::commit)\n    // \"precedes\": nearest descendant tag = roots(tags() & commit::)\n    let revset_str = if ancestor {\n        format!(\"heads(tags() & ::{})\", commit_hex)\n    } else {\n        format!(\"roots(tags() & {}::)\", commit_hex)\n    };\n\n    let tag_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;\n    if tag_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Ok(None);\n    }\n\n    // Find the tag name for the first result\n    let tag_id = &tag_ids[0];\n    let view = repo.view();\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.tags() {\n        if local_remote.local_target.added_ids().any(|id| id == tag_id) {\n            return Ok(Some(name.as_str().to_string()));\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(None)\n}"
}

> DEVELOPER

One issue "Bookmarks" isn't matching "Branches"
Bookmarks: main, main@git, main@origin
Branches: main, remotes/origin/main

The remotes/origin/main makes more sense coming from git, thougths?

> AGENT

You're right that gitk shows git's native ref format (`remotes/origin/main`). But jj uses its own ref model — `main@origin` is the canonical jj way to express a remote-tracking bookmark, and `main@git` is the colocated git ref.

I'd say keep `Bookmarks` (not `Branches`) since this is a jj tool, but change the format to be more useful:

- `main` — local bookmark
- `main@origin` — remote tracking (jj native format, clearer than `remotes/origin/main`)
- Drop `main@git` — it's a jj internal implementation detail for colocated repos, always mirrors the local bookmark, and adds noise

So for this commit: `Bookmarks: main, main@origin`

Sound right?

> DEVELOPER

sg I verified that `jj -r main@origin` and `vc-x1 -r main@orgin` works

> AGENT

`Bookmarks: main, main@origin` — `main@git` is gone. Ready when you are.

> DEVELOPER

ATM you are not showing Bookmarks if it's empty, but on gitk it's Branch(es) is alway present as we do for Follows Precedes we should do the same. One other related difference, we only list Bookmarks if the commit is the current location of the bookmark, on gitk all commits on a Branch list that branches name, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good observations. Two questions:

**Always show Bookmarks:** Easy, agree — consistency with Follows/Precedes.

**Branch membership vs bookmark pointer:** This is a fundamental difference between git branches and jj bookmarks. In git, every commit "belongs to" a branch (it's on the branch's ancestry line). In jj, a bookmark is just a pointer to a single commit — there's no "membership" concept.

We *could* emulate gitk's behavior by showing bookmarks where the commit is an ancestor: `bookmarks() & commit::` would give all bookmarks reachable from this commit. But that could be misleading — in jj, bookmarks move independently and a commit being an ancestor of `main` doesn't mean it "belongs to" main the way git thinks about it.

My recommendation: keep the current behavior (show bookmarks that point directly at this commit) but always show the line. If you want the git-style "which branches contain this commit" view, that could be a separate field like `Contains:` or a `--branches` flag. But mixing the two semantics in one field would be confusing for anyone who understands jj's model.

What do you think?

> DEVELOPER

I thought that might be the case, but when we push it to "github" or other service bookmarks are branches, correct?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. When you `jj git push --bookmark main`, jj exports the bookmark as a git branch. On GitHub, `main` is a branch. The git remote has no concept of jj bookmarks — they're branches all the way down.

So the ancestry question ("is this commit on main?") is perfectly valid from the git/GitHub perspective. And since vc-x1 uses colocated repos with git remotes, the git mental model matters.

I'd say: show it. Use the revset `branches() & commit::` approach — for each bookmark, check if the commit is an ancestor. Label it `Branches:` since that's what they are on the remote, and it matches gitk.

We could keep `Bookmarks:` as the "points directly at" line if needed later, but for now `Branches:` with ancestry semantics is more useful and matches gitk.

Want me to go ahead with that?

> DEVELOPER

y

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "old_string": "fn format_bookmarks(commit: &Commit, repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>) -> String {\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {\n        // Local bookmark\n        if local_remote\n            .local_target\n            .added_ids()\n            .any(|id| id == commit.id())\n        {\n            names.push(name.as_str().to_string());\n        }\n        // Remote bookmarks (skip \"git\" — internal colocated ref)\n        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {\n            if remote_name.as_str() == \"git\" {\n                continue;\n            }\n            if remote_ref.target.added_ids().any(|id| id == commit.id()) {\n                names.push(format!(\"{}@{}\", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    names.join(\", \")\n}",
  "new_string": "fn format_branches(\n    commit: &Commit,\n    workspace: &Workspace,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n    let view = repo.view();\n    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n\n    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {\n        // Check if commit is an ancestor of this local bookmark\n        for bookmark_id in local_remote.local_target.added_ids() {\n            let revset_str = format!(\n                \"{} & ::{}\",\n                commit_hex,\n                bookmark_id.hex()\n            );\n            let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;\n            if !results.is_empty() {\n                names.push(name.as_str().to_string());\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Check remote bookmarks (skip \"git\" — internal colocated ref)\n        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {\n            if remote_name.as_str() == \"git\" {\n                continue;\n            }\n            for bookmark_id in remote_ref.target.added_ids() {\n                let revset_str = format!(\n                    \"{} & ::{}\",\n                    commit_hex,\n                    bookmark_id.hex()\n                );\n                let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;\n                if !results.is_empty() {\n                    names.push(format!(\"{}@{}\", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(names.join(\", \"))\n}"
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "old_string": "fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {\n    let desc = commit.description();\n    println!();\n    if desc.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"    (no description set)\");\n    } else {\n        for line in desc.lines() {\n            println!(\"    {line}\");\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}",
  "new_string": "fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {\n    let desc = commit.description();\n    // Skip the first line (title) — already shown on the Ids line\n    let body: String = desc\n        .lines()\n        .skip(1)\n        .skip_while(|l| l.is_empty()) // skip blank line between title and body\n        .collect::<Vec<_>>()\n        .join(\"\\n\");\n\n    if body.is_empty() {\n        println!(\"Description:\");\n    } else {\n        println!(\"Description:\");\n        for line in body.lines() {\n            println!(\"    {line}\");\n        }\n    }\n    println!();\n}"
}

> AGENT

That's great — Follows/Precedes working with real tags, multiple parents on a merge commit, and `Changed files:` is correctly empty for a merge with no tree changes. Performance looks fine too on the linux repo.

> DEVELOPER

It was instant on this recent commit, the 71st commit out of 1,428,481. It took gitk about 52 seconds to complete reading the 1.4m commits, about the same time as `jj git init`.

> AGENT

That's the advantage of jj-lib's approach — it resolves the specific commit and its immediate neighborhood (parents, children, tags) via revsets and the index, without loading the full DAG into memory. gitk has to read everything upfront to build its display.

For a tool like `show` that operates on one commit at a time, this is ideal. The question will be when you start building something that needs to traverse larger portions of the graph — but even then, revsets with the index should keep things fast.

Want to do the finalize steps for 0.18.1, or are there more tweaks you want to make?

> AGENT

0.67 seconds to show the initial Linux commit — which has ~17,000 added files — on a 1.4M commit repo. The tree diff had to enumerate every single file in that initial snapshot, and it still completed sub-second. The hash-based short-circuiting we discussed earlier is irrelevant here (no parent tree to skip against), so this is pure tree enumeration speed from jj-lib's Rust implementation.

For comparison, a typical commit touching 5-10 files would be effectively instant, which you already saw with the merge commit.

   196→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   197→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   198→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   199→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   200→
   201→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   202→        .unwrap_or_default()
   203→        .with_timezone(
   204→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   205→        );
   206→
   207→    dt.format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").to_string()
   208→}
   209→
   210→fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {
   211→    let desc = commit.description();
   212→    // Skip the first line (title) — already shown on the Ids line
   213→    let body: String = desc
   214→        .lines()
   215→        .skip(1)
   216→        .skip_while(|l| l.is_empty()) // skip blank line between title and body
   217→        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   218→        .join("\n");

   224→        for line in body.lines() {

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "old_string": "    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);\n\n    println!(\"Changed files:\");\n    for entry in diff_iter {\n        let diff = entry.values?;\n        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n\n        // Skip tree (directory) entries — only show files\n        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n            || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n        {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string();\n\n        match (before, after) {\n            (None, Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"    Added {} {path_str}\", file_type_str(&value));\n            }\n            (Some(_), None) => {\n                println!(\n                    \"    Removed {} {path_str}\",\n                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),\n                );\n            }\n            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {\n                println!(\"    Modified {} {path_str}\", file_type_str(&value));\n            }\n            (None, None) => {\n                // Conflicted or both absent — skip\n            }\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);\n\n    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for entry in diff_iter {\n        let diff = entry.values?;\n        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();\n\n        // Skip tree (directory) entries — only show files\n        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n            || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))\n        {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string().to_string();\n\n        let line = match (before, after) {\n            (None, Some(value)) => {\n                format!(\"    Added {} {path_str}\", file_type_str(&value))\n            }\n            (Some(_), None) => {\n                format!(\n                    \"    Removed {} {path_str}\",\n                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),\n                )\n            }\n            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {\n                format!(\"    Modified {} {path_str}\", file_type_str(&value))\n            }\n            (None, None) => continue,\n        };\n        lines.push(line);\n    }\n\n    println!(\"Changed files: {}\", lines.len());\n    for line in &lines {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }"
}

I was thinking ~ for infinity although that's also a NOT as is !. Related to that `jj` uses `-n` for the short form of --limit, we should add `-n`

> AGENT

How about just no limit flag means show all (current behavior), and `-n 50` means cap at 50? Then the default is "everything" and you only limit when you want to. The first/last split when truncating is a nice touch.

For the flag: `-n` / `--limit` with the split display when truncated.

Or do you want a default cap of 50 that you override with `-n 0` for none (show all)?

> DEVELOPER

I never want 17k so visually, may want to grep through all so I think a default of 50 is more than enough, eventually we'll add a config and the user can change it to any in as well as infinity

> AGENT

Makes sense. So:

- Default: 50
- `-n <count>` / `--limit <count>`: override
- `-n 0`: show all (no limit)
- When truncated: first N/2, `... (X more) ...`, last N/2
- Positional: `vc-x1 show <rev> [file-count]`

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", args.revision).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let commit_id = &commit_ids[0];\n    if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n        return Err(\"cannot show root commit\".into());\n    }\n\n    let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n\n    print_header(&commit, &workspace, &repo)?;\n    print_description(&commit);\n    print_diff(&commit, &repo)?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    // Positional rev overrides -r if -r is still default\n    let rev = if let Some(ref pos) = args.pos_rev {\n        if args.revision == \"@\" {\n            pos.as_str()\n        } else {\n            args.revision.as_str()\n        }\n    } else {\n        args.revision.as_str()\n    };\n\n    // Positional limit overrides -n if -n is still default\n    let file_limit = if let Some(pos_n) = args.pos_limit {\n        pos_n\n    } else {\n        args.limit\n    };\n\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, rev)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{rev}'\").into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let commit_id = &commit_ids[0];\n    if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n        return Err(\"cannot show root commit\".into());\n    }\n\n    let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n\n    print_header(&commit, &workspace, &repo)?;\n    print_description(&commit);\n    print_diff(&commit, &repo, file_limit)?;\n\n    Ok(())\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

But wait — `show` is fundamentally a single-commit command. `@..` means "ancestors of @" which is multiple commits. Should `show` iterate over multiple commits like `desc` does, or should the `..` notation not apply here?

For `show`, the `..` notation with a count makes sense as "show this commit and N ancestors" — print each commit's full show output. But that's a bigger change. 

The simpler fix: strip `..` and just use the bare rev, treating `@.. 2` the same as `@ 2` (ignore the dots, the second positional already means file count not commit count).

Actually that's confusing — in `show`, the second positional is file count, not commit count. So `vc-x1 show @.. 2` would mean "show @ with 2 files max" which is different from `vc-x1 desc @.. 2`.

I think `show` should just reject `..` notation for now since it's a single-commit view. Parse the dots off and warn, or just make it work by stripping them. What's your preference?

> DEVELOPER

I do want to support .., have show have 3 positional parameters rev, commit count, file count. Maybe file count 0 == none, !0 = infinity, thoughts

Makes sense. So: `vc-x1 show [rev] [commit-count] [file-count]`

- `vc-x1 show @.. 3` — show @ and 2 ancestors, default 50 files each
- `vc-x1 show @.. 3 0` — same but no file list
- `vc-x1 show @.. 3 !0` — hmm, `!0` would be parsed as a string not a number

For the infinity sentinel, how about:
- `0` = no files (suppress)
- No arg / `50` = default 50
- `-1` or some large number = all

Actually `-1` won't work as positional (looks like a flag). What about:
- `0` = no files
- Default = 50  
- `all` as a special string? Or just use `-n 0` for all via the flag?

Wait, you said earlier `0` for show all. Let me reconsider. You want:
- `0` = none (suppress file list)
- `!0` = all (infinity) — but `!` is shell-hostile

How about swapping it:
- `0` = all (current behavior, matches existing `-n 0`)
- `-n` flag default 50
- To suppress: some other mechanism

Or just keep `-n 0` as "show all" and add a separate sentinel for "no files"? This is getting overloaded for a positional. Maybe the positional file-count only makes sense as a flag and the 3rd positional isn't worth it?

> AGENT

Right, `resolve_dot_args` handles that — `@.. 3` becomes `::@` with limit `3+1=4`. So show just needs to use the same `resolve_dot_args` machinery as `desc`/`list`/`chid`, then iterate over the resulting commits calling `print_header`/`print_description`/`print_diff` for each.

For file count, let's keep it as just the `-n` flag (not a positional) to avoid ambiguity with the commit count positional. So:

- `vc-x1 show @.. 3` — 4 commits, 50 files each (default)
- `vc-x1 show @.. 3 -n 0` — 4 commits, all files
- `vc-x1 show @-` — 1 commit, 50 files

That way positionals match the other subcommands exactly, and `-n` is show-specific for file limiting. Sound good?

> DEVELOPER

I think -n 0 means none and the "Changed files:" and the extra "\n" are not present. Other choices for all how about `-n all` no ambiguity their?

> AGENT

Clean. So:

- `-n 50` (default) — show up to 50 files
- `-n 0` — suppress file section entirely
- `-n all` — show all files

That means `-n` can't be a `usize` — needs to be a string we parse ourselves. Let me implement it.

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use std::sync::Arc;
     3→
     4→use chrono::Offset;

     6→use jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;

     8→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
